Cross-examination by Shri Sunil Sen .Organising Secretary. 

* * » * « 

It is also correct that during the months of April, 1970, May, 1970, June, 1970, 
July, 1970, August 1970, October, 1970 and December, 1970, 211, 163, 137, 242, 

194. 197 and 232 ordinary wagons were supplied and all those wagons were manual- 
ly loaded. The witness volunteers that in all those months ordinary wagons 
were supplied only against short rakes and as such as already stated earlier 
the numoor of such wagons being less, mechanical loading had been stopped and 
manual loading was resorted to; 

* * * « 4 

To arbiiratur . — No record has been maintained to Indicate the number of 
wagons which were supplied to the colliery with defective brakes. The percen- 
tage of supply of such defective wagons is very cratic, it varies from 10% to even 
cent percent, 

* * « « * Ijl 

Notwithstanding the existence of the third machine which was lying idle the 
management considered it prudent not to get rid of some of the casual wagon 
loaders to avoid labour unrest, As the incidence of breakdown in the maebinea 
Is also very cratic it is not possible to precisely indicate the requirement of per- 
manent wagon loaders even if the management is given free hands to utilise 
both the machines, The third machine which is not In use cannot be used and 
has not been u.sed so far because for putting it into commission some additional 
Installations are to be made. It is very difficult to precisely indicate the number 
of days on which the machine remains idle during the month because there had 
been no breakdown at all in some months but there had been months when there 
had been frequent breakdowns arid In the first quarter of 1970 one of the 
machines was under breakdown, so far as I remember, for over two months.” 

. The union has not produced any documentary evidence. The only witness, 
Shri Bancha Mondol CWW.I). Wagon Loader of Bankola Colliery examined by 
the union has not denied the allegations of the management against the wagon 
loaders regarding forcible manual loading of those wagons which were meant 
for mechanical loading. The witness has also not contradicted the statement of 
WW.I in this regard. In the circumstances, I place reliance on the statement of 
Shri S. K. Singh (MW. I). Shri Bancha Mondol (WW. I) has admitted that, ‘‘in 
Bankola Colliery loading work Is being done manually as well as mechanically 
and this system of manual as well as mechanical loading has been in existence 
since long. When I joined the colliery in 1062 this was in existence.” Admitted- 
ly two machines for loading coal Into wagons are in existence at the colliery for 
pretty long time. They have not been introduced presently by the paanagement 
to jeopardi.se the interest of the workmen in any way. Hence I am fully con- 
vinced that the normal capacity of these two machines to load coal into wagons 
cannot be ignored while determining the strength of the permanent wagon load- 
ers at the colliery specially when the supply of wagons by the railways in ad- 
mittedly very cratic and their loading is time bound by the railways, 

7. I give below the table showing the number of ordinary as well as box 
wagons loaded at the colliery during the year 1970, monthwise, prepared on the 
basis of exhibits M.l(a) to M.l(d): —

5lc. 3(ii)] THE GAZETTE OF INDIA: APRIL 24, 1971/VArSAKHA 4. 1893 2101 


This shows that on an average 181 and 623 ordinary and box wagons respectively 
were loaded at Bankgla Colliery on an average per month during the year 1970. 
Admittedly there are 26 working days in a month in a coal mine and ns such the 
numbers ot ordinary and box wagons loaded at the colliery work out to 7 and 24 
per day respectively. The capacity of two machines is to load twenty box wagons 
per day. This will be evident from Exts. W.l(c) which shows that on 18th July, 
1970, 24th July, 1970, 31st July, 1970 and 18th August, 1970, 19-1|2, 20,213, 

19-112 and 19-1 12 box wagons were loaded mechanically. This ha:s not been ch^- 
lenged by the union by producing either oral or documentary evidence. So after 
keeping apart 20 box wagons per day for mechanical loading, only seven ordinary 
wagon.s and four box wagons are left out to be loaded manually. In accordance 
v^'ilh the directions of the Labour Appellate Tribunal an ordinary wagon can be 
loaded by five persons in eight hours. This work load has also been up-held by 
the Central Wage Board Recommendations for the Coal Mining Industry vide 
paras 92 and 53, Chapter VIII, Section ‘C’ of the Coal Wage Board recommenda- 
tions. During the course of hearing it was adihitted by both parties that on an 
dveiago the capacity of ordinary and box wagons are 22 tons and 66 tons respec- 
tively. Accordingly five persons are required to load ordinary wagons and twelve 
persons are required to load box wagons during eight hours. On this basis 35 
loaders are required for loading ordinary wagons and 48 wagdn loaders ai’e re- 
quired to load four box wagons (24-20). Thus 83 wagon loaders are required for 
loading the balance wagons left out for manual loading. Provision is to be made 
for ab.sentceism, leave, sick, etc. In Chapter XV, Section 13 of their recom- 
mendations it has been observed by the Central Wage Board for the Coal Mining 
Industry as under: — 

"Or the average number of wagons requiring loading per day multiplied by 
the numper of loaders required for each wagon. Add to that margin 
of leave and absenteeism which is about 22 per cent,” 


Thus for this purpose 22 per cent of the required number of wagon loaders rre 
to be provided for which works out to 18 wagon loaders. Provision is also to be 
made for meeting the contingencies of supply of defective wagons and breakdown 
of machineries. The MW.l has admitted that the number of defective wagons 
supplied to the colliery is eratic but varies between 19 per cent to cent per cent. 
Regarding the incidence of breakdown of machineries he has stated that, “it is 
very difficult to precisely indicate the number of days on which the machine 
remains idle during a month because there had Keen no breakdown at all in some 
months but there had been months when there had been frequent breakdowns 
and in the first quarter of 1970 one of the machines was under breakdown, so far 
as I remember, for over two months.” There is nothiffg on record to precisely 
indicate anything in this regard. However, after careful consideration I have 
come to the conclusion that It would be reasonable to provide for on additional 
strength of 24 wagon loaders capable of loading two box wagons, to meet the 
contingencies of supply of defective wagons and break-downs of machines. Thus 
In my opinion 125 (83 plus 18 plus 24) should be the strength of permanent wagon 
loaders of Bankola Colliery. 


8. In the circumstances, I hold tha4) the number of permanent wagon loaders 
at Bankola Colliery of M/s. The Burraikur Coal Co. Ltd., P.O. Ukhra, Dlst. Bur- 
dwan shall be 125. TTie Arbitration award is made accordingly and submitted to 
the Central Government .under Section lOA of the Industrial Disputes Act, 1947. 

(Sd.) K. Sharan, 

Regional Labour Commissioner (09, Asansol & Arbitrator,

ARBITRATION AWARD 

The envPloyers in relation tp the Bararee/Jealgora collieries of Messrs East 
Indian Coal Company Limited, Post office Jeaxgora, Dhanbad and their workmen.. 
represented by the Bihar Coal Miners’ Union Digwadih, Dhanbad have by the 
written agreement dated the 3rd August, 1970 in pursuance of the provisions of .sub- 
section (i) of Section lOA of the Industrial Disputes Act, 1947 agreed to refer tba 
industrial dispute existing between them to the ’ arbitration py me and forwarded 
a copy of the said arbitration agreement to the Central Government- The Central 
Government, Ministry of Labour, Employment and Rehabilitation issued the noliflca- 
tion.No, 8/133/70-LH. II d&led 5th December, 1970 and published the said arbitra-, 
tion agreement at page 5574 of the Gazette of India, Part II, Sec. 3(ii) dated 12th 
December, 1970 in persuance of sub-sections (3) of Section 10 of the Induatrial 
Disputes Act, 1947. The workmen were represented before me by Shri Pritish, 
Chanda, President, Bihar Coal Miners Union ahd Shri D. L. Sengupta, Advocate 
and the employers by Shri S, S. Mukherjee, Executive Committee Member. Rani- 
ganj Chamber of Commerce. On admission by the employers. Ext. W-1 lor the 
workmen and on admission by the workmen. Ext.s, M 1 and M 2 for the employers, 
were marked. A witness was examined for each party. 

2. The memorandum of settlement between the parties in Form H is Ext.' W. 1 
and the . Arbitration agreement entered Into by them Is published at page 5574 of 
the Gazette of India, Port II, Section 3(il) dated 12th December, 1970. As per the 
settlement and agreement the specific matters in dispute are as foUows; — 

“(i) What should be the rate of payment for working on a Sunday, the weekly 
day of rest, In respect of the workmen of the Engineering Department 
of the Bararee and Jealgora collieries of M/ s East Indian Coal Co. 
Ltd., 

(ii) Whether the demand of the workmen for payment for working on Sunda.y. 
weekly day of rest, at the rate of normal wages lor the day plus ii 
time extra is justified?” 

Admittedly, Sunday is the weekly day of rest for the workmen of the Engineer- 
ing Department of Bararee and Jealgora collieries of the employers. The case of 
the workmen i.s that the workmen keep the da.v apart exclusively for domestic 
work, social life and rest and as such if a workman Is called upon to work on 
a Sunda.y there is complete dislocation of his planned life which cam be compensated 
only Jf he is paid extra wages for the day equivalent to 2i times the daily wages, 
l.e. IJ time extra In addition to his normal wages for the day. The contention of 
the employers is that the workmen of the Engineering Department, whose work is 
not for a period beyond 48 hours In a week Including work on a Sunda.y Is only 
legally entitled to the normal wages alone for the date and not even any overtime 
wages, far less the normal wages plus li time extra. At the same timt the 
employers have pointed out that as a matter of fact th^ are paying at If times 
their normal wages to the workmen for working on a Sunday. According to the 
employers there is no justification or basis for the claim put forth by the workmen - 
Neither hi the pleadings nor in the evidence led by them the parties have clarified 



Sec. 3(u)J THE GAZETTE OF INDIA; APRIL 24, 1971/VAlSAKHA 4, 1893 2103 


whether the workmen of the Engineering Department of the Bararee and Jealgora 
co.lieries of the employers are underground or surface workmen. I proceed with 
tne presumption that aU of thorn are surface workmen. It is also not clajinecl in 
the pleadings that whether they are monihly rated or daily rated workmen. But 
W W. 1 says that the ^^'crkmen consist of monthly rated and daily rated. Ip deter- 
n'.inlng the d'spute it i>; true that 1 have to he.ar in mind the inconvenience sullcrtd 
by the workmen when they are made to work on a Sunday, which is their weekly 
rest aay and tncy Keep it apprt exclusively for domestic work, social life, etc. But. 
al the same time I should not also forget that my rinding in this respect will have 
repercussion-j on other industrial concerns though not directly and my. ilndings 
a; c not binding on them. I should also bear in mind that the .elfeci of m.y award 
should be aimed at industrial peace and not to sow germ of future litigation or 
disputes in the industrial sphere. The parties have not brought any material on 
record to show Ihe iiractice prevalent in ather collieries in respe ;t ot payment ol 
Wages on Sunday, si when the workmen are called upon to work on that day. WW. t 
has simply stated that in TlSCO collieries monlh'y rateil v/j'kmen arc getting 1* 
nays salary extra for each Sunday on which they work in ni-diuoi'i to wh.it they 
get on monthly basis and the daily rated workmen li a day’s wages for each 
Sunday they work. This oral testimony has no support, 

3, In respect of payment for work on holidays there was an agreement early in 
1943 between the Indian Mining Association, Indian Mining Federation and the 
Indian Colliery Owners Association on the one hand and labour in the coal mining 
Industry on the other. This is generally known as Joshi A,greement. 
This agreement was adopted and embodied in paragraph 6 of the standing, 
orders for the coal mining industry which wore certified on 8th April, 1950. 
A copy of the certified standing order is Ext. M. 1. The overtime rates both under 
the ".Ijshi Agreeaient' and standing orders were to be in all cases IJ times the 
n'jrrpai rates of wages. In the Indian Mines Act chen lu force there was no pro- 
vl.s.lon for ovenime payment. In the Act which came into operation in 1&52 Seclion 
33 provided for extra wages for overtime and it increased the overtime rates for 
the under ground workers. But this provision has no beaiing on the preser.t case, 
Ina.smuch as the workmen concerned in the present case are all surface werkmetj. 
Again the question of payment of extra wages for working on paid festival holiday.'i 
was an i.^sue in the Award of the All Indian Industrial (Colliery Disputes) and 
the Decision of the fjabour Appellate Trlbuiral of India. But nedher the Award 
nor the Decision has affected paragraph 6 of the certified standing orders as far 
a.s wages of surface workmen working on a Sunday, a weekly day of rest is con- 
cerned. Paragraph 0 of the certified standing -orders for the coal Mining industry, 
Ext. M 1 runs thus ; 

“Weekly and dally rated employees shall receive over time payment at 11 
times the normal daily rate for work on the weekly rest day, recognis- 
ed holidays and for overtime work at other days. Those monthly paid 
employees who are entitled to receive payment shall receive overtime 
payment at the rate of li times of l/26th of their monthly basic wage 
inclusive of dearness allowance for work on the weekly rest day 
recognised at the cohiery”. 

As I have pointed out earlier the over time rates as per the Standing orders and 
‘Joshi Agreement’’ are, in all cases, li times the normal rates of wages. It Is an 
admitted position that for workmen oi daily rate Sundays arp off days without 
wages. So, as per the standing orders If a daily rated workman -works on a Sunday, 
the weekly rest day, he gets only Jj times of his iiormal dally rate Of work for 
the day. To be more clear, if his normal daily rate of work is Hs. 5/-, for having 
worked on a Sunday, the weekly rest day, he gets only Rs. 7.50 and nothing more. 
On the other hand, if a monthly rated workman works on a Sunday, the weekly 
rest day, he gets his wages for the day at the rate of l/26th of his monthly wages 
plus li times of the same. That means. If his monthly wages are Rs. 26/- for 
working on a Sunday, the weekly rest day, he gets (26:26) Rs. 1/- plus Re. 1.50, 
total R-s. 2,50. The certified standing orders. Ext. M 1 are still in force and para- 
graph 6 thereof is not shown to have been affected by any subsequent award or 
agreement. Section 33 (i) of the Mines Act, 1952 is substituted by a new sub- 
section by virtue of Section 17 of the Act, 1962 of 1959, whereby if a person works 
In a mine, whether above ground or below ground he is, In respect of the overtime 
work done by him beyond 48 hours In any week, entitled to wages at the rate of 
twice his ordinary rate of wages. But, as I have pointed out above, as per the 
standing orders, Ext. M 1 If a monthly rated workman works on a Sunday, a weekly 
rest day. he gets his wages for the dacf at the rate of l/26th of his monthly wages 
plus li times of the same, l e. total of 2i times of his normal dally wage, whereas 
as per the amended section 33 of the Mines Act he gets only twice his normal daily 
wage. As pointed out by WW. 1, out of about 200 workmen of the Engineering 



il04 the gazette of INDIA: APRIL 24, I971/VAISAKHA 4, 1893 [Part II— 


Department ol Bararee and Jealgora collieries 170 or 180 are monthly rated em- 
ployees. Application of the present Section 33 of the Mines Act will not be bene- 
ficial to the majority of the workmen working In the Engineering Department ot 
Bararce and Jealgora collieries. Of course, daily rated workmen are to get twice 
their normal daily wage under the provision of the Mines Act and only li times 
of thpir normal wage under standing orders, Ext. M 1, because for the daily rated 
workmen Sunday, a weekly day of rest Is a holiday without wages. Further, Sec- 
tion 33(1) pf the Mines Act speaks only about the overtime done by a workman 
beyond 48 hours in any week and does not speak specifically for working on a 
Sunda,y, a weekly re.st day. In the absence of any other material I presume that all 
-collieries are making payment to their workmen in accordance with paragraph 6 
of the standing orders for working on a Sunday, the weekly rest day, and I do 
not see any reason why a deviation should be made in the case of Bararee and 
Jealgora collierie.s of the employers. Thi.s extra payment is in consideration of 
the inconvenience caused to the workrhen for working on the weekly rest day and 
1 consider this extra payment as sufficient compensation. 

4- T, therefore, find that in respect of the Engineering Department of the Bararee 
and Jealgora collieries ol M/s East Indian Coal Company the rate of payment to 
workmen for working on a Sunday, the weekly day of rest, should be (J) to the 
monthly rated workman his wages for the day at the rate of l/26th of his monthly 
wages, inclusive of his basic wage and dearness allowance plus IJ times of the 
same: and (2) to the daily rated workman 14 times of hl.s normal dally wage in- 
clusive of his basic wage and dearness allowance. 

The settlement, Ext. W. 1 is marked by consent of parties. A,s per para (3) of 
the settlement, I hold this award wlli be effective f.-nin Ist June. 1970. The award 
is made accordingly and submitted to the Central Government under sub-isection(4) 
of Section 10-A of the Industrial Disputes Act, 1947. 


(Sd.) N. Venkata Eao,

AWARD 

The above reference is under sub-section (2) of section 10 of the Industrial 
Disputes Act and arises out of order No. 28/50/68-LRIlI of the Central Govern- 
ment dated the 9th July, 1968. By the said order the following dispute has been

dlfVig- 30?ITIJ^, 1969 I 

RWT 12 (l4)/70-lTHo mTo — 4, 
cTlfliJ 22Rf, 1970 I

26 1970 ! 



Sec. 3(ii)] THE GAZETTE OF INDIA: APRIL 24, 1971/VAlSAKHA 4, 1893 2107 


^referred to this Tribunal: ‘‘Whetber the demand of the union that all the members 
of the Crew should be paid overtime and not on Individual basis if there is any 
work during recess hours said If overtime is given on individual basi.s then those 
of them who ate not given overtime should be permitted to leave the vessel is 
justifled? If so, what should be the relief? 

2. The relevant facts are briefly stated below. The port of Calcutta is one 
of the major part,s governed by the Calcutta Port Act, 1890. The Commissioners 
for the Port of Calcutta are a body corporate constituted under the said Act. 
The Commisisoners being a local authority the Minimum Wages Act, 1948 became 
applicable to the Commis.sioners and the minimum wages were fixed in respect of 
certain categories of the Commissioner’s employees including those employed in the 
crane vessels. 

3. The workmen concerned are attached to the following crane vessels of the 
Calcutta Port Commissioners: 

(1) C.V. “Atlas” 

(2) C.V. “Vbbahu” 

(3) C.V. “Mahabahu” 

(4) C.V. “Washington" 

(.11 C.V. “16 Ton Pon toon Crane” 

4. The first three are self-propelled vessels and the last two are non-propelled 
floating craft, A self-propelled vessel moves about under Its own power whereas 
a non-propelled vessel has got to be towed from place to place. 

.I. The workmen attached to the crane vessels “Atlas”. "Virbahu” and “Maha- 
bahu” work In two shifts whereas single shift working l.s In vogue in “Washington 
and ‘TO Ton Pontoon Crane”. The crane vessels work at different points of 
the Docks and the river according to the requirement of the Commissioners for 
loading and un-loading of heavy-lift cargoes or liffihg materials, machinery, machi- 
nery parts etc, 

6. Till 30th April, 1954 the crew attached to all crane vessels excepting 'the 
“16 Ton Pontoon Crane" which was then known as “20 Ton Floating Crane” 
were granted overtime for four hours in each shift which wa.s of 12 hours duration. 
The crew of the “16 Ton Pontoon Crane” (then known as 20 Ton Floating Cranel 
followed workshop hours, i.e., from 7-30 A.M. to 4-30 P.M. with a recess of 45 
minutes from 12-:i0 P.M. to 1-15 P.M. They were entitled to overtime only if they 
worked beyond 18 hours a week and overtime was at ordinary rate. 

7. After the Minimum Wages Act had come into force requiring the Com- 
missioner for the Port of Calcutta to pay overtime at twice the ordinary rate for 
work beyond 9 hours a day or 48 hours a week to the members of the crew of the crane 
vessels, they decided to introduce three shifts In those of the vessels where the 
crew worked in double shifts with a view to eliminate overtime, Learning the 
decision of the Comml-ssioners in regard to the matter, the crew of the crame 
vessels represented to the Commissioners in February, 1951 that the old system 
of payment of 4 hours overtime in shifts of 12 hours duration should continue, 

■3. As a result of prolonged negotiation and correspondence between the Com- 
missioners and the Union, it was Anally decided that from the 1st May, 1964, the 
crew of the crane vessels would be paid overtime for 2 hours dally at twice the 
ordinairy rate and 2 hours’ fixed recess, while they would be on 12 hours’ shifts, 
as the crew of comparable vessels in the Marine Department were entitled to 2 
hours’ recess in a 12 hours’ shift, such recess being mutually arranged- 

9, After the introduction of third shift in the Cargo Docks, the crews of the 
crane vessels booked for traffic workihg complained of frequent loss of recess and 
In reply to a representation by the Union for granting them an additional hour’s 
ovetime, the Chief Mechanical Engineer by his letter dated oOth August, 1955 
siatd as fchi.ws: ‘ Regarding additional overtime, 1£ this I 3 to be on a general b,isis. 
administrative sanction will be necessary but in eases where the crew of any 
particular crew vessel could not be given recess they are being given overtime 
for that period,” 

10. The question of recess hour for the staff of crane vessels was discussed by 
the Chief Mech.anlcal Engineer with the representatives of the staff concerned and 
tte General Secretary of the Port Shramik Union In or afbout March 1958. The 

4 



2108 the Gazette of INDIA: APRIL 24. 1971/VAISAKHA 4, 1393 [Part 


following recess hours for the staff were suggested by the Chief Mechanical 
Engineer; 

For the Day Shift 

10-30 A.M. to 11-30 A.M. 

3-00 P.M. to 4-00 P.M. 

For the Night Shift 

10-00 P,M. lo 11-30 P.M. 

5-00 A.M. to 6-00 A.M. 

The .staff of the crane vessels at that time worked in two 12 hour shifts, namely 
6 A.M. to 6 P.M. and 6 P.M. to 6 A.M. The representative® of the Union agreed 
to accept the above timing with the exception of the recess period from 5 A-M- 
to 6 A.M. for the night shift staff. The Chief Mechanical Engineer overruled 
their objection to the recess period from 5 A.M. to 6 A.M. because he was of 
opinion that the timings for recess were to suit traffic work and that it was 
necessary to introduce the above mentioned timings to suit traffic work. He 
therefore Introduced the above mentioned timings for recess with effect frona 
the 11th of March, 1958 (vide Ext. M-1). 

11. During the discussion it wasi also pointed out by the Chief Mechanical 
Engineer that the men must continue to work during recess period if, for any 
reason, a loading or unloading work was in progress and that they must con- 
tinue till the job was completed. He further pointed out that as the movement 
of the vessel from place to place was dependent on Dock Master’s prcgranmme, 
the recesa period should be adjusted so that the shipping programme might not 
be hampered. He assured that If one particular recess hour could not be taken 
due to pressure of work, the staff would be entitled to overtime payment as per 
Rules (vide, Ext. M-1). 

12. The present dispute originated sometime in 1965. The version of the 
workmen Is as follows. In the year 1965, the union stated receiving complaints, 
about denial of overtime to individual members of the crew though the parti- 
cular vessels to which such crew members were attached were engaged In work 
during recess hours (vide, parg 14 of the Statement by the workmen). The 
workmen’s point of view as expressed In their letter dated November 26, 1965- 
to the Chief Mechanical Engineer (vide Ext. M-2) was follows: “Some men who 
are not paid overtime for work during recess hours are not permitted to leave 
the vessels during such hours. The crews of the vessel work as a team and our 
agreement with the Administration governing payment of overtime to them is 
cm collective basis. Our repeated representations on this subject have gone 
unheeded. The financial loss suffered by the men concerned shpuld be made 
good and in future overtime payment should be made In accordance with normal 
rules applicable to the crews of all vessels under the Commissioners.’’ (vide 
Ext. M-2). 

13. The matter was taken up by the Union for discussion with the Chief 
Mechanical Engineer on the 9th of December, 1965 and the minutes of discusison 
on the subject as recorded by the Chief Mechanical Engineer read as follows: 
“Non-payment of ove time to certain members of the crew of all crane vessels 
for working during recess hours: 

“Shri M, L, ChatterJee stated that the Chief Mechanical Engineer was violating 
the agreement with the Administration governing payment of overtime to the 
crew of crane vessels. The men who are not being paid overtime for work during 
recess hours should be permitted to leave the vessels during such hours. 

“C.M.E. stated all agreements were being honoured by him. Enough provi- 
sions have been made In the vessels for the crew to spnd their rece.ss hours 
on board the vessels. However, those who were not booked for overtime work 
during rece.ss hours could leave the vessels with the permission of the Engineer- 
in Charge keeping in view the safety of the vessels” (vide Ext. M-3). ' 

14 . Thereupon the members of the crew who were not paid overtime though 
their ' vessels worked during recess hours approached their Englneer-in-Charge 
for permission to leave the vessel during recess hours. The Engincer-m Charge 
refused permission to the members of the crew to leave the vessel and issued 
the following order in writing; 

“As per the C M.E.’s order by phone no body can leave the vessel during 
recess period, otherwise will be punished and charge sheeted. 

15 The Union on receipt of the information appraised the Chief Labour Offi- 
cer of the Calcutta Port Commissioners of the development and addressed a 


Sec. 3(ii)j THE GAZETTE OF INDIA: APRIL 24, 1971A^AIS,^KHA 4, 1893 2109 


letter to him, being letter No. 429/ 1202/E dated 24th December, 1966 confirming 
the telephoiiic discussion with him and Incorporating the copy ol the order men- 
tioned in the pevious paragraph. 

16. Thereafter the Union took up the matter with the higher authorities and 
ultimately agreed to submit the Issue for adjudication under section 10(2) of the 
Industrial Disputes Act, when no settlement was possible. It is in this way 

the present dispute has been referred to this Tribunal by the Central Government 
Under section lOf-T) of the said Act. 

17. The main point for consideration is whether all the members of the craw 
should be paid overtime if there is any work during recess hours. The expres- 
sion "If there is any work during recess hours” really means in the context of 
the facts giving rise to the present dispute "if any crane vessel Is requisitioned for 
work during recess hours”. The workmen object to the payment of overtime 
only to some ol the members of the crew, leaving out the rest. This method of 
payment of overtime to some and not to aU la what they call payment on Indi- 
vidual basis. It is contended op their behalf that the crew of a vessel work as 
a team and not on individual basis and that consequently their hours of work 
are ccwnmcn and no veuiatlon can be made in such hours of work in respect of 
individual members of the crew booked for work In a shift. They further con- 
tend that in no vessel of other department of the Calcutta Port Commissioners 
discrimination is made between individual members of the crew In regsnrd to 
their hoUrs of work and that all rnembers of the crew working in a ship or vessel 
are given overtime for equal number of hours or at the same consolidated rate 
of overtime work. 

18. As stated above the crew of the crane vessels ato allowed 2 hours of 
fixed recess in each shift. It Is contended on behalf of the workmen that as the 
hours of recesai for the crew are fixed Ini crane vessels all members of the crew 
should be on recess during the same period and that all of them should bo paid 
overtime If such recess cannot be allowed to aU of them at a time. Another 
ground taken by them in support of their claim is that all agreements, under- 
standing with the imlon in regard to the payment of overtime to the members 
of the crew provide for payment of overtime to the crew of particular type 
of vessels on upiform basis and not on Individual basis. 

19. The Commissioners too concede that the members of the crew of Oie 
crane vessels have to work in a team. But they qualify this concession by saying 
that on self-propelled crane vessels, different groups of workers employed in 
the vessels carry out work in a team when the vessel Is being shifted from Dock 
to Dock or from Berth to Berth, or when loading and unloading operations are 
being carried out; and that similarly on non-prepelled vessels also different group 
of workers are required to work during the operations detailed above. 

20- The position taken up by the Commissioners on this point wlE be clear if we 
refer to para^aph 7 of the statement filed by them. Part (li) of Para 7 runs thus: 
When the self-propelled vessels are shifted from Dock to Dock or from Berth to 
Berth, the following workers of the crew generally are required to do the work: 
(I) Inland Master, (li) Seacunny, (iii) DocfcLascan-s, (v) Engine Driver, (vi) Engine 
Room Tindal, (vll) Greaser, (vilj) Coal Tremer, (lx) Electrician (only on occasions 
on ‘Virbahu’ and ‘Mahabahu’)”. Part (Ul) of Paragraph 7 is to the following 
effect; “When the self-propelled vessels' are carrylne on actual loading and un- 
loading operations, the following workers of the crew are required to do work; 
(i) Crame Driver, (ii) Winchman, (Hi) Lascars, (iv) Electrician (whenever provid- 
ed), (b) Fireman, (vi) Greaser, (vil) Engine Driver (In case of any break-down). 

21. Annexure ‘A’ to the Written Statement filed by the Commissioner, s mentions 
the stall employed on the various crane vessels Including the Pontoon Crane. By

30. Tho foregoii’p' dlscu.s.';ion makc.s it abundantly clear that the demand of the 
union tha^ all the members of the crew .should bo nal'd overtlrne and not on indi- 
vidual b.-iSi'; if there is an.y work during reces.s hours is perfectly ju.stifled. It li. 



2112 IHB gazette of INDIA; APRIL 24, 1971/VAlSAKHA 4, 1K93 [Pajit II— 


therefore, not necessary for me to decide whether the other demand of the union, 
namely, that those of them who see not given overtime should be permitted to 
leave the vessel, is Justified. Moreover the materials on record ore not sufficient 
to give any decision either way. There is' nothing on record to show that the 
members of the crew are entitled to leave the vessel even when there is no ques- 
tion of working during recess. According to the Flarbour Master, every Inland 
vessel should be fully manned and ready to act in catse of emergency and aU the 
crew are to act in case of emergency. It is' an oSence if they are not on their 
vessels, This seems to Indicate that the crew cannot leave the vessel as of right 
even when they are not required to work during recess hours; it that be so, It h; 
all the more reason why they should not be sdlowed to leave the vessel when It 
Is requisitioned for loading or unloading during fixed recess hours. However, I 
do not come to any definite finding on this point, nor Is it necessary for me to do 
90. I may however point out that In this case those who were not paid overtime 
sought for permission to leave the vessel, but that permlsslrni was refused. This 
strengthens the claim of the workers that all the members should be paid overttnie 
if there is' any work during recess hours. 

31. My award la that the demand of the union that aU the members of the 
crew slu>uld be paid overtime and not on individual basis if there Is any work 
during recess hours is Justified and! that overtime is to be paid on this basis from 
the date of the award. 


32. A copy of this award should be sent to the Central Government under 
Section 15 of the Industrial Disputes Act.

tion by the Central Government. 


DRAFT SCHEME

tion of Employment) Amendment Scheme, 1971, 

2. In the Madras Unregistered Dock Workers (Regulation of Employment) 
Stdjeme, 1937, — 

(I) In clause 14-A, for item (a) of sub-clause (5) the following item shall 
be substituted, namely:^ — 

"(a) Where a worker has been suspended pending enquiry, he shall be paid 
for the first ninety days from the date of suspension, a subsistence 
allowance equivalent to one-half of the dally wage Including dearness 
and other allowances and thereafter the Chairman In exceptional cases, 
grant higher subsistence allowance not exceeding three-fourths, of such 
dally wage including dearness and other allowances: 

Provided that where such enquiry Is prolonged beyond a period of ninety days 
for reasons directly attributable to the worker, the subsistence allow- 
ance shall, for the period exceeding ninety days, be reduced to one- 
fourth of the dally wage Including deamesg and other allowances.”; 

(11) In clause 14-AA for Item (ill) (a) of sub-dause (21 the following Item 
shall be substituted, namely; — 

“(111) (a) Where a worker has been suspended pending enquiry, he shall be 
paid for the first ninety days from the date of suspension, a subsis- 
tence allowance equivalent to one-half of the dally wage Including

S O. 1701. — Whereas the Central Government Is of opinion that an indua- 
trial dispute exists between the employers in relation to the Messrs. Peter and 
Smith (India) Private Limited, Caleutta and their workmen in ro.spcrct of the 
matters specified in the Schedule here to annexed; 

And whereas the Central Government considers it desirable to refer the said 
dispute for adjudication; 

Now, therefore, in exercise of the powers conferred by clause (di of sub- 
section (1) of section 10 of the Industrial Disputes Act, 1947 (14 of 1947). the 
Central Government hereby refers the said dispute for adjudication to the Central 
Government Industrial Tribunal, Calcutta, constituted under section 7A of the 
said Act.

S-O. 1702. — Whereas M/s, Toy Menka Kalsha Limited, No, 198, Jamshedjl 
Tata Road, Bombay-1 (hereinafter referred to as the said establishment) have 
applied for exemption under clause (a) of sub-section (1) of section 17 of the 
Employees’ Provident Funds Act, 1952 (19 of 1962); 

And whereas in the opinion of the Central Government, the rules of the 
provident fund of the said establishment with respect to the rates of contribution 
are no less favourable to the employees therein than those specified in section 6 
of the said Act, and the ' employee? are also in enjoyment of other provident fund 
benefits which on the whole are not less favourable to the employees than the 
benefits provided under the said Act or under the Employees’ Provident Funds 
Scheme, 1952 (hereinafter referred to as the said Scheme) in relation to the 
employees in any other establishment pf a similar character; 

Now, therefore, In exercise of the powers conferred by clause (a) of sub-section 
(1) of section 17 of the ^d Act. and subject to the conditions specified In the 
Schedule annexed hereto, the Central Government hereby exempts the said establ- 
ishment from the operation of all the provisions of the said Scheme, and in pursu- 
ance of sub-section (3) of the said Section 17, the Central Government hereby 
directs that,— 

(a) the employer in relation to the said establishment shall pay within fifteen 
daya of the close of the month to the Employees’ Provident Fund, 



Sec- 3(ii)] THE GAZETTE OF INDIA: APRIL 24, 1971/VAISAKHA 4, 1893 2I2J 


inspection charges at the rate of 0.09 per cent (zero point zero nine 
per cent) of the pay (basic wages, dearness allowance, retaining 
allowance, if any, and cash value of food concession admissible there- 
on) for the time being payable to the employees of the said establish- 
ment who would have become mernbens under the said Scheme but 
for this exemption; 

(b) the said employer shall invest the provident fund contributions in 
accordance with the directions igsued by the Central Government 
from time to time. 


The Schedule 

1. The employer ahaU. submit such returns to the Regional Provideni Fund 
Commissioner as the Central Government may, from time to time, prescribe. 

2. The employer shall furnish to each employee an Annual Statement of Account 
or Pass Book. 

3. All ei^nsee involved In the administration of the Fund including the mato- 
tenance of accounts, submission of accounts and returns, transfer of accumula- 
tloaii, payment of inspection charges etc., shall be borne by the employer- 

4. The employer shall display on the Notice Board of the establishment a copj 
of the Rules of file Fund as approved by fiie appropriate Government and, as aod. 
when amended alongwlth a translation of the salient XKitnts thereof In the language 
of the majority of the employees. 

5. Where an employee who Is already member of the Employees Provident 
Fund (Statutory Fund) or the Provident iTind of another exempted estabUghment 
is employed In his establishment, the employer shall immediately enrol him as 
a member of the Fund of the establishment, and accept the past accumulations 
In respect of such employees and credit to his account. 

6. The employer shall enhance the rate of provident fund contribution appro- 
priately if rate of provident fund contributions tor tbe class of establlriimwts 
in which bis establishment falls is enhanced under the Employees’ Provident Funds 
Act, 1952 so that the benefits under the provident fund Scheme of the establish- 
ment shall not become less favourable than the benefit provided under the 
Employee’s Provident Funds Act, 1952. 

7. The estabUshment shall submit an audited balance sheet of its provident 

fund every year to the Regional Provident Fund Commissioner within 3 months 
of the close of the year. ' ' 

8. No amendment of the Rules of the provident fund shall be made without the 
previous approval of the Central Proiddent Fund Commissioner. Where any 
amendment Is likely to affect adversely the Interests of the employee, the Central 
Fhnvident Fund Commissioner shall, before giving his approval, give a reasonable 
opportunity to the employees to explain their point of view,

2124 GAZETTE OF INDIA; APRIL 24, 1971A^AISAKHA 4, J893 [Part II— 


(Department of Labour and Employment) 

New Delhi, the 15th April 1971 

S.O. nos.'— In pursuance of section 17 of the Industrial Disputee Act, 1047 (14 
of 1047) the Central Government hereby publishes the following Award of the 
Central Government Labour Court, Delhi in respect of a complaint under section 
33A of the said Act filed by Shrimati Sarla JaSn, Pharmacist, Employees State 
Insurance Corporation Dispensary, MehrauU, New Delhi, which was received by 
the Central Government on 7th i^rll, 1971. 

BEFORE THE CENTRAL GOVERNMENT LABOUR COURT, DELHI 
Present; 

Shri R. K. Baweja, Central Government Labour Court, Delhi. 

3l3t March, 1071/lOth Chait, 1892(S) 

Complaint U/S 33A of the Industrial Disputes Act, 1947 
Complaint I.D. No. 1 of 1971 

..Smt. Sarla Jain W/o Shri R. K. Jaim Pharmacist, E.S.I. Corporation Dis- 
pensary, Mehrauli, . New Delhi C/o Hospital Enmloyees Union, H.O. 
Aggarwal Bhavan, G.T. Road, Tees Hazari, Delhi. — Complaint. 


Verffus 

Employees State Corporation, Kotla Road, New Delhi. — RcspoTident. 

Shri C. P. Aggarwal — for the complainant. 

Shri Jaswant Rai — fctr the respondent. 

In the Matter of LCA No. 68 of 1970 
AWARD 

The complainant Smt. Sarla Jain filed an application under section ^(2) of 
the Industrial Disputes Act, 1947 (LCA No. 68 of 197(», in this Coijrt and claimed 
hotiBe rent allowance for tJie period from 1st March 1970 to 30th April 1^0, 
which she alleged had been discontinued by her employer, the employees State 
Corporation, Kotla Road, New Delhi, (hereinafter to be referred as respondent). 
That application is stUl pending in this Court. It is alleged in the complaint filjM 
by her that the respondent Issued orders on the 11th of February, 1971 wherein 
a direction was issued lor the recovery of the whole amount paid to toe com- 
plalnant by way of house rent allowance prior to 1st Msnrch, 1970. Th® com- 
plant further continued that during the pendency of the application under sc^ 
tion 33C(2), the respondent passed the order of recovery of rent in violation of 
the provisions of section 33 of the Act. It was. therefore, prayed that the com- 
plaint be dealt with according to law and the respondent be restrained fr<™ talc- 
ing any action against her for recovering the smd amount till the final msww 
of her application filed under section 33C(2) of the Industrial Disputes Act, 1947, 

2 The respondent in its reply inter olia raised the objection th^ as was 

no industrial dispute between the parties pending before tWs court, it could not 
be said that the provisions of section 33 of the Act had been contravened ^d 
that the complaint under section 33A of the Act was not maintainable. A 
Joinder was also filed on behalf of the complainant m which it was ^terated 
that an industrial dispute between the parties existed m view of the pendency 
of the application under section 33C(2) of the Act. 

s Tt WHS admitted bv Shri Aggorwal who represented the complainant tlmt 
no®ef"eIce " section 10 of t& Act had been made by the fov^ent f^ 
adjudication of an industrial dispute existing between the parties. The relevant 
portion of Section 33 of fhe Act reads as follows: — 

“During the pendency of any conciliation proceedmg ® 

(fificer or a Board or of any proceeding ® 

Tribunal or National Trlbimal in respect of an industrial dispute, no 
employer shall — 

fal in regard to any matter connected with the dispute, alter, to the pre- 
^ ^ jidice of the workmen concerned in such dispute the conditions of 
sem^ce applicable to them immediately before the commencement 
of such proceeding; or 




Sec. 3(ji)] THE GAZEITE OP INDIA; APRIL 24, I'OI/VAlSAiaiA -I. 1893 2125 


(b) for any misconduct connected with ttie dispute, discharge or punish, 
whether by dismissal or olhorwisc, any workmen concerned in such 
dispute. 

Save with the express permission in writing of the authority before 
which the proceeding is pending." 

Section 33A lays down that where an employer contravenes the provisions of 
section 33 during the pendency of proceedings before a Labour Court, Tribunal or 
National Tribunal, any employee aggrieved by such contravention, may make a 
complaint in writing, in the prescribed manner to .such Labour Court, Tribunal 
or National Tribunal and on receipt of such complaint that Labour Court, Trlbuoal 
or National Tribunal shall adjudicate upon the complaint' as if it were a dispute 
referred to or pending before it, in accordance with the provisions of this Act and 
shall submit its award to the appropriate Government and the provisions of thi.s 
Act shall apply accordingly. So. It is crystart clear from a perusal of the above 
provisions that it i' necessary'for the complainant to prove that there* was a i en- 
dency of an industrial dispute and that during that pendency the employer has 
altered the conditions of service to his prejudice and has discharged, punished or 
otherwise terminated his services for any misconduct without the prior permission 
of the Tribunal or Board or the Labour Court concerned, If there is no industrial 
dispute pending then the provLsions of sections 33 and 33A will not be applicable. 
An application under section 33C(2) enables an employee to file his claim in the 
Labour Court .specifically empov/ered for that purpose for the recovery of money 
due from an employer. Such an application does not embrance within its sweep 
any industrial dispute. The expression “industrial dispute'’ has been defined jn 
section 2(k) of the Act and when it arises between an employer and his em- 
ployee, IT Ls referred lor adjudication by the Government as provided in section 
10 of the Act. It cannot be referred direct by an employee to any Court or 
Tribunal. In my view, therefore, the complaint under section 33A i.s not main- 
tainable as no industrial dispute between the parties is pending before any Ijabour 
Court or Tribunal. Shri C. P. Aggarwal who represented the complainant, how- 
ever. relied on a dispute between M. Ramasubhu and Kani Motor Works, Tiru- 
nelveli and another (1964, l.LLJ. 249). I fail to understand as to how this ruKng 
helps the complainant. 'The question before the Madras High Court in that ca.se 
was whether an employee in proceedings under section 33C(2) of the Act could 
raise an objection regarding the engagement of a I'-iwyer by the emploj'Cr within 
the meanina: of section 36(4) of the Act. Tt was held that a proceeding under 
section S3C(2) of fhe Act involved a dispute within the meaning of sub-section 
(4) of Section 30 of the Act. It is to be noticed that the word used in that sub- 
section is “dispute" and not an “industrial dispute”. So. when an employee flics 
an application against his employer that he i.s entitled to certain benefits, which 
can be computed in terms of money, certainly there is a dispute but it is not an 
industrial dispute of which we And a reference in section 33. Every dispute is 
not an industrial dispute. I am, therefore, clear in my mind that the complaint 
under section 33A of the Act is not maintainable. The objection of the respondent 
Is upheld and the complaint is di.smissed. 

(Four pages) 

31st March, 1971. 

(Sd.) R. K. Baweja,

S.O- 17^4. — Whereas the applications specified in the Schedule annexed hereto, made 
under clause w) of sub-section (2) of section 33 of the Industrial Disputes Act, 1947 (14 of 1947) 
arc pending before Shri S. C. Barua, presiding Officer, Industrial Tribunal, Dibrugarh ; 

And whereas the services of Shri S. C. Barua have ceased to be available; 

And whereas the Central Government has constituted an Industrial Tribunal with Shri O.N. 
Borah as the Presiding Officer with headquarters at Dibrugarh ; 

Now. therefore, in evtreise of the powers conferred by sub-section (l) of section 33B 
of the said Act, the Ctuttal Government hereby withdraws the proceedings inrelationtothesaid 
applications from S. C. Barua and transfers the same to Shri G. N. Borah, Presiding Officer, 
Ind istrialTribttal, Dibrugarh for the disposal of the said proceedings with the direction that the 
saidTribunal shailproceed with each of the said applications from the stage atwhichit Istrans- 
ferred and dispose of the same according to law.

Act, 1949, as relate to person managing any of the banks specified in 
the schedule to this notlflcatjon sh^ not apply to a director on the 
Board of the Industrial Reconstruction Corporation of India Limited, 
ai company formed and registered under the Companies Act, 1956- 
(1 of 1956); and 

(b) that the provisions of sub-section (3) of section 19 of the said Act shall 

not apply to any of the banks specified in the said Scnedule in so 
far as the said provisions relate to the holding of shares in the said 
Industrial Reconstruction Corporation of India Limited.

(1) The State Bank of India; 

(II) any subsidiary bank as defined In clause (k) of section 2 of the Stale 

Bank of India (Subsidiary Banks) Act. 1959 (38 of 1959),

ing Companies (Acquisition and Transfer of Undertaking) Act. 1970 
(.5 of 1970);

(Department of Revenue and Insurance) 

Schedule I 

(Income-tax Department) 

Bombay, the 24th July 1970 

S O. 1734.- -Whereas the Central Government is of opinion that It is neces- 
sary and expedient in public interest to publish the names and other particulars- 
relating to the assessees on whom a penalty of not less than Rs. 6,000/. was 
imposed: — 

(a) for the concealment of Income during the period commencing with. 

1st April, 1969 and ending with 31st March, 1970. 

(b) for the failure to file the returns of income or to produce the books 

of accounts during the period commencing with 1st April, 1069 and^ 

ending with 31st March, 1970. 

(c) for the non-payment of tax during the period commencing with 1st 

April, 1069 find ending with 31st March, 1970. 

And has therefore, in exercise of the powers conferred by section 287 of ihm 
Income-tax Act. 1961 (43 of 1961), directed that the names and other particulars 
of the a-ssessees aforesaid be published the same are hereby published in Sche- 
dules I, II & III, hereto annexed.

concealment of Income during the period commencing with Jst April, 1969 and' 
ending with 31st March, 1970 where no appeal was presented within the time- 
allowed therefor, or where the appeal against the penalty imposed has been 
filed, has become final during the financial year 1969-70.

S. O. 1736. — Whereas the Central Government is of opinion that it is necessary and expedirtSt 
in prbllc interest to publish the names and other particulars relating to the assessees on whom a 
penalty ofnot less than Rs. 3000/- was imposed. 

(<j) for the coricealment of Income during the period commencing with 1st April, 1969 and 
ending with 3 1 St March, 1970. 

(b) for the failure to file the returns of income or to produce the books of accounts during 
the period commencing with ist April, 1969 and ending with 31st March, 1970. 

(e) for the non-payment of tax during the period commencing with ist April, 1969 and 
ending with 31st March, 1970. 

And has therefore, in exercise of the powers conferred by section 287 of the Income-tax Act, 
igSl (43 of 1961), directed that the names and other particulars of the assessees aforesaid be 
published, the same arc hereby published in Schedules I, II & III hereto annexed.
